## 3 Decisions and open items
Dispositions follow `ANSWERS.md` (2026-09-20): **CONFIRMED** (owner answer) · **KEEP** (default stands, with the stated clarification) · **REVISED** (corrected before the affected task) · **PROVISIONAL** (engineering estimate, not a measured optimum; tunable) · **DEFERRED EVIDENCE** (experiment defined now, result only from live evaluation). No row is `OPEN`.

| ID | Question | Resolution | Gates |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-01 | Group/root package/artifact prefix | **CONFIRMED** `io.astrolabe` / `astrolabe-*` (domain ownership confirmed). Registry verification and publishing credentials are release work, not authorized by this. | P0.1.1 |
| D-02 | JVM target | **CONFIRMED** JDK 26 for build toolchain, Java/Kotlin targets (`--release 26`, `-Xjdk-release=26`), Gradle daemon, tests and supported runtime; no Java 17/21 compatibility work; JDK 27 not used (Gradle/Kotlin support stops at 26); JDK upgrades are deliberate version boundaries. | P0.1.1, P1.12.3 |
| D-03 | SQLite access | **KEEP** `sqlite-jdbc`, explicit SQL, small tx/migration helper; `foreign_keys=ON` per connection; durability and transaction boundaries per D-44; blob publication before the referencing transaction. | P0.5.1 |
| D-04 | Git access | **KEEP** `git` CLI with a temporary index and expected-old-id ref updates; Git 2.20 is a proposed minimum to *test*, not a substitute for feature tests; raw recovery snapshots bypass content conversion (D-53). | P0.6.2 |
| D-05 | Search backend | **REVISED** ripgrep with a documented common-subset JVM fallback: literal vs regex mode, supported regex syntax subset, ignore rules, hidden/binary files, Unicode/case rules, symlink policy; unsupported patterns fail explicitly; zero matches / partial / failed / denied remain distinct. | P0.6.3 |
| D-06 | Token estimation | **REVISED** heuristic estimates (bytes/3.6 with code-aware tweaks; jtokkit optional) are for *planning*; dispatch uses a profile-specific admission contract recording estimator id/version, exact vs estimated, uncertainty margin and headroom, including effective history and every serialized contribution; unknown continuation size ⇒ fresh lineage or a capacity result (I-17). | P0.3.2, P2.3.3 |
| D-07 | Async API style | **REVISED** `suspend`/`Flow` inside; `io.astrolabe.java` facade **and** Java-implementable inbound SPIs (`JavaProviderAdapter`, `JavaAuthority`, `EventSink`) with defined future cancellation, exception mapping, callback threading, resource closure and bounded event delivery; a Java-authored adapter and authority are tested (I-14). | P0.3.4, P0.4.2, P1.9.6, P1.12.3 |
| D-08 | Identity types | **KEEP** validated public `data class`es; no value-class mangling or Kotlin `Result` in the Java surface; immutable collections/defensive copies at authority boundaries; ids validated non-empty/canonical. | P0.2.1 |
| D-09 | First language/runner set | **PROVISIONAL** order Python → JS/TS → JVM, all three represented in P1 fixtures; implement one end-to-end runner slice (Python) first; `node --check` never claims TypeScript validation (project TS checking belongs to its declared checker); missing required runner ⇒ `unavailable`; unsupported inline syntax ⇒ `not_run`. cargo/go/dotnet/mocha/unittest in P3.1.4. | P1.6.6, P1.7.2, P3.4.2 |
| D-10 | Inline syntax mechanism | **KEEP** cheap language CLI where available; tree-sitter later (both ERROR and MISSING nodes count); accepted file types and parser completeness declared; a syntax receipt concerns the exact file version and is never behavioural acceptance; otherwise `not_run`. | P1.6.4, P5.4.1 |
| D-11 | Confined runner backend | **KEEP** trusted-local implementation, `ConfinedRunner` contract only; if a host *requires* confinement, configuration/dispatch fails rather than substituting trusted-local; the execution label describes limitations, it enforces nothing. | P1.10.2 |
| D-12 | Platforms | **CONFIRMED** Windows and Linux equal support targets, OS-contract tests from P0.6.1 on both; current hands-on testing is on Windows; the native process-control mechanism is named and tested before job-object/process-group semantics are claimed (D-43); macOS and other modes unsupported until tested. | P0.6.1, P1.12.4 |
| D-13 | Environment fingerprint (`env_id`) | **REVISED** versioned canonical fingerprint: OS/arch, resolved tool/checker/parser identities and versions, relevant effective environment values as protected digests (names alone are insufficient), build flags, lock/dependency state, runner policy id, external fixture identities; unknown relevant inputs prevent cross-candidate reuse. | P1.2.2 |
| D-14 | Redaction | **KEEP, stricter boundary** configurable regex set + env allowlist, heuristic and labelled; redaction limits recorded per observation; raw logs never exposed through recall/export unredacted; signed opaque provider items never rewritten into invalid replay artifacts; exact preimages in protected recovery storage; redacted ranges grant no coverage (D-49). | P1.10.3 |
| D-15 | State location | **REVISED** external durable per-project root, host-configurable, keyed by canonical repository identity (D-44); no `.git/info/exclude` mutation; worktrees share the project store with distinct `workspace_id`s; authoritative state never in a disposable cache and never inside the source tree ([§4](docs/state/contracts.md#sec-4)). | P0.5.1 |
| D-16 | `select_shape` size classes S/M/L | **PROVISIONAL** S: ≤ 3 expected files ∧ 1 requirement ∧ one package; L: cross-package ∨ > 10 expected files ∨ ≥ 4 requirements; else M. Risk, required review, authority and unavailable capabilities override file counts; incomplete discovery is `unknown`, never low risk; recalibrate only from outcome evidence. | P2.2.1 |
| D-17 | Contract digest ≤ 150 tokens | **REVISED** bounded digest of the *current authorized objective* (not always the first request): objective excerpt, obligation ids/status/currency, critical exclusions; deterministic truncation labels even when the first sentence exceeds the cap; full acceptance definitions stay mandatory in `[K]` and review packets (D-52). | P1.1.4 |
| D-18 | `greedy_cover` admission | **KEEP** deterministic greedy after mandatory content; unit = note ∪ transitive `depends_on` bundle with cycle detection; marginal added tokens priced so shared dependencies count once; mandatory content that cannot fit ⇒ rescope / larger profile / capacity result; a bounded heuristic, not an optimal-cover claim. | P2.3.1 |
| D-19 | Failure-fingerprint normalization | **REVISED** normalize only identified volatile data (hex ids, timestamps, temp paths, addresses); preserve workspace-relative path/module, error code and meaningful literals; keep a coarse `ErrorSignature` separate from the full attempt `Fingerprint` (error + attempted fix + state + requirement) so a changed fix cannot erase repeated-error detection. | P4.6.2 |
| D-20 | Model-facing tool schemas | **REVISED** seven logical families with local mask enforcement always; a schema is frozen only after its adapter/profile/role lineage validates it (supported dialect and capability behaviour recorded); masking preferred where valid ([§15.1](docs/platform/adapters.md#sec-15-1) makes universal masking conditional on provider correctness); a new lineage may select a supported schema or return an explicit unsupported-profile result; schemas never change mid-session (I-24). | P1.6.1 |
| D-21 | MCP mounts | **KEEP** mount/catalog contracts in core, MCP networking deferred; server annotations are hints; local approval and capability validation decide effects and dispatch rights; descriptor text is data. | P4.7.1 |
| D-22 | Second-model calls (judge, probe, extractor, repair) | **KEEP** one adapter contract with routed helper profiles and fakes; helper calls consume the originating budget and obey cancellation/evidence rules; a scripted judge tests protocol only, never judgment quality or savings. | P4.4, P4.5 |
| D-23 | Review before review cells exist (Stage C) | **KEEP and make reachable** `Authority.review` satisfies a *required-review capability* in P2–P3 with sequential execution, recorded as a substitution; it never implies probes, routing or repair helpers; no available required reviewer ⇒ `blocked` (I-03). | P2.2.1, P3.5.2 |
| D-24 | KB Markdown front matter | **KEEP** fixed export-only YAML subset with deterministic quoting/escaping, no YAML parser; SQLite canonical; no Markdown import/recovery path — typed records are recovered, Markdown is for views and golden tests only. | P2.6.1, P1.5.1 |
| D-25 | Serialization | **KEEP with versioning** `kotlinx.serialization` JSON with schema version and provider/model/protocol provenance on every persisted record; native provider items as opaque `JsonElement` replayed unchanged only on a compatible lineage; explicit task/evidence data transfers across providers; protected native replay storage and redacted presentation are separate views. | P0.3.1, P0.5.2 |
| D-26 | Concurrency and process ownership | **REVISED** structured coroutine lifetimes (`SupervisorJob` per campaign), bounded parallel reads, serialized workspace mutation; a process-local mutex excludes nothing outside the process, so the baseline is **one controller process per project store** enforced by an OS file lock (D-44), with S3 workers inside that owner; cancellation performs bounded evidence/reservation cleanup and never loses late effects; slow event listeners never hold mutation or database locks. | P0.5.1, P1.6.2, P1.9.4, P0.4.1 |
| D-27 | Test identity per runner | **REVISED** namespaced runner-native identities: (check, project/module, file/suite, name, parameterization) as needed; duplicates preserved with multiplicity, never overwritten; ambiguous matching cannot prove pre-existing failure or equivalence (D-50). | P1.6.6, P1.7.5 |
| D-28 | Comparators B0/B-HELM and all live gates | **DEFERRED EVIDENCE** offline harness now; every live gate recorded `UNMEASURED` with prerequisites; fakes cannot satisfy B0/B-HELM, memory, routing or S3 gates; completion levels per §0.2 (I-19). | P6, P7 |
| D-29 | Logical cache breakpoints | **KEEP, qualified** S/R/K/T breakpoints are logical hints; the adapter maps supported mechanisms, limits and minimums or uses uncached operation if policy permits; fake cache behaviour is a deterministic simulation, not a prediction of provider hits; billable cache-write classes preserved (I-16). | P0.3.2, P0.3.5 |
| D-30 | Result ids and dedup | **REVISED** campaign-global persisted `#n` aliases (never reset per cell) mapped to canonical journal/artifact ids, allocated transactionally incl. children, never recycled, with context/workspace provenance; `look` dedup requires semantic request equivalence and sufficient live coverage, not `(what, target, version)` alone (D-46; I-06, I-07). | P1.6.1, P1.6.3, P2.4.4 |
| D-31 | S0 default write scope | **KEEP with authority semantics** repository-local scope minus protected defaults (`.git/`, CI config, lockfiles, migrations dirs); scope bounds possible writes, it authorizes no unrelated work; already-authorized CI/lockfile/migration changes belong in the committed contract, not behind a blanket prompt; basic enforcement exists in S0 (P1.7.8, I-02). | P1.1.2, P1.7.8 |
| D-32 | Rules-file trust | **REVISED** discovery (`.astrolabe/rules.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`) only *proposes* candidates; host configuration or explicit user authority binds canonical path + digest + provenance before the snapshot becomes instruction text; otherwise the file stays repository data; an approved snapshot is reused across resumes; changed bytes do not inherit approval (I-08). | P1.3.2, P1.10.1 |
| D-33 | Anchored hunk semantics `{anchor, near?, new}` | **KEEP, clarified** exact matching first; then a tightly specified whitespace normalization (blank runs collapsed, trailing blanks ignored, line endings normalized) with an explicit mapping back to original-byte spans; apply `near` (a literal within 20 preceding lines) to select among candidates, then require exactly one non-overlapping span inside currently displayed coverage; `new` replaces the anchored span (insertion = anchor + inserted text); semantically ambiguous whitespace/string cases refuse with candidates rather than choosing. Removing normalization would be a spec amendment ([§9.1](docs/runtime/workspace-editing.md#sec-9-1)). | P1.6.4 |
| D-34 | `risk_floor` tier mapping | **REVISED** declared `packet.risk` maps explicitly (`blast_radius ≥ 3` ∨ reversibility = hard ∨ declared high ⇒ `high`; `blast_radius = 2` ⇒ `medium`); contract touch and fan-in (≥ 20 ⇒ `high`, ≥ 5 ⇒ `medium`) supplement it and can never erase a high-risk obligation whose dependencies were not discovered; function and risk floors re-applied after every adjustment (I-23). Versioned with the tier table. | P4.5.1 |
| D-35 | `adjust_with_calibration` | **PROVISIONAL, automatic demotion OFF** promotion to a higher tier allowed after ≥ 20 logged quadruples for the feature class with > 30 % verified failure; demotion requires comparable *verified outcomes for the proposed cheaper tier* from a frozen/shadow evaluation — sample thresholds alone never authorize it; never below `max(never_below, risk_floor)`. | P4.5.1 |
| D-36 | Harness-origin KB notes vs "curator is the sole publisher" | **KEEP** one serialized `KbWriter` with explicit authority per operation: harness STATUS records are same-task checkpoints (`admitted_by: harness`, lint-exempt, never injected elsewhere); the curator alone publishes reusable admitted knowledge; shared plumbing never turns a model proposal into a harness fact. | P2.4.3, P2.6.1 |
| D-37 | Injection score weights and threshold | **PROVISIONAL** `w_scope = 3, w_dep = 2, w_fresh = 1, w_use = 1, w_evid = 1, w_len = tokens/500`, threshold 2.0, with bounded feature definitions/ranges declared before use; staleness is an eligibility check before ranking; mandatory CON/invariant content bypasses optional caps but counts toward total admission; tuned from held-out usefulness and failure data. | P4.1.3 |
| D-38 | Role policy text ownership | **KEEP** frozen, versioned SDK default texts ([Appendix A](docs/reference/kernel-contract.md#sec-appendix-a) for implementing/writer; [§3.4](docs/architecture/roles-shapes.md#sec-3-4) duties for others) with host overrides that change wording only, never executor authority or acceptance obligations; mandatory shared policy is preserved even beyond a cosmetic line count; text version recorded in attempt and compile fingerprints. | P1.8.1, P2.1.2, P4.4.6, P3.7.1 |
| D-39 | `ambiguous_bug` and `measured slack` | **PROVISIONAL** `ambiguous_bug` = defect-class request with no reproducible failing acceptance `run:` at open, or ≥ 2 plausible causes recorded by the plan cell (no reproducible failure is *uncertainty*, not proof of a defect); `measured slack` = remaining budget ≥ 1.5 × the sequential-S1 estimate including reservations, review, verification, integration and an uncertainty margin, in consistent units, and the parallel-cell limit not exhausted; logged as `select_shape` inputs; `1.5×` cannot enable S3 before its promotion. | P2.2.1, P5.1.4 |
| D-40 | Impact pre-scan at campaign open | **REVISED** candidate paths = paths named in the request ∪ lexical hits of request identifiers ∪ atlas hubs of the focus subsystem; the pre-scan is *incomplete discovery*: record its coverage, unresolved dependencies and explicit risk; no hits never establishes no contract impact; refresh when actual touched symbols/paths become known and stop/upgrade before a now-disallowed action (I-23). | P3.2.6 |
| D-41 | Transform jail before a confined backend exists | **KEEP with a firm limit** trusted-local transforms run only when trusted-local mode is authorized; a post-hoc diff is not a jail and cannot disprove outside/network effects; inventories, preimages, expected counts, honest effect status and required checks/review are the controls; a confinement requirement returns `unsupported` until the P7 backend exists. | P3.3.1 |
| D-42 | Calibration prior before the KB path exists | **KEEP** deterministic P2 `CalibrationStats` block (optional prior, off until its ablation) and the later admitted `CAL` note path; never inject both; model-written CAL text is never treated as measured statistics; provenance, versioning and decay retained. | P2.6.4, P4.2.1 |
| D-43 | Process ownership backend (I-20) | **REVISED/NEW** `ProcessOwner` via JDK FFM (`java.lang.foreign`): Windows job objects (kill-on-close, breakaway denied) and POSIX process groups (`setsid`/`setpgid`, `killpg`); durable log capture to store-owned files with cursors; terminal record written by a supervisor independent of the requesting coroutine; five distinct outcomes: observation timeout (FX-22, harness alive), execution deadline, cancellation request, confirmed termination, lost/unknown; processes do **not** survive harness death by default, so after a crash their handles resolve `lost` and are never relaunched blindly; a detached/survivable mode is a separate later capability with its own fixtures. | P0.6.1, P1.6.5, P2.2.4 |
| D-44 | Storage root, ownership, durability (I-21) | **REVISED/NEW** root = `<host-configured or OS user-state dir>/astrolabe/projects/<repo-identity>/` with `repo-identity = digest(root commit id, canonical repository path)`; shared by all worktrees of the repository with workspace-qualified records; `ProjectLock` (OS file lock) enforces one controller process per project; SQLite `foreign_keys=ON`, WAL, `synchronous=FULL`; blob bytes and directory fsynced before the referencing transaction commits; bounded orphan-blob recovery; unsupported flush/rename guarantees fail closed; recovery coverage stated per platform: process termination always, OS/power loss only where fsync semantics hold. | P0.5.1 |
| D-45 | Tested inputs and receipt currency (I-04) | **REVISED/NEW** a receipt carries `tested_inputs` (paths@versions from its declared closure ∪ observed access) and `input_stability ∈ {exclusive, isolated, unknown}`: `exclusive` = the workspace mutation lock is held for the whole check and a post-check rescan detects any subprocess write; `isolated` = an exported candidate whose manifest is verified before and after; a required acceptance receipt is eligible only with `exclusive|isolated` and no relevant input mutation during the check; mutation during a check keeps the factual outcome but makes the receipt ineligible for the final tree (rerun); `stampAfter == now` is necessary, not sufficient; declared scratch/output policy prevents spurious invalidation. Spec refinement of [§8.4](docs/verification/scheduler.md#sec-8-4) (§3.1). | P1.7.4, P3.1.5 |
| D-46 | Evidence aliases and dedup (I-06, I-07) | **REVISED/NEW** `Alias` = campaign-global monotonically allocated `#n` → canonical journal/artifact id, transactional, never recycled on rebuild/cancel/resume, with context/workspace provenance; `look` dedup key = normalized request (`what`, target, range, `in`, `glob`, `near`, `since`, budget class, workspace, index version) ∧ sufficient *live* coverage in the current projection; an evicted or incomplete earlier result never satisfies a broader request. Spec refinement of [§5.4](docs/runtime/tools.md#sec-5-4) (§3.1). | P1.4.1, P1.6.3, P2.4.4 |
| D-47 | Workspace path contract (I-09) | **NEW** `WorkspacePath` used by every built-in filesystem operation (look, edit, registry, stamps, closures, ownership): canonical real-path resolution; traversal and absolute escapes rejected; mutation through symlink/junction/reparse ancestors rejected until an explicit operation supports it; case/alias identity unified on case-insensitive filesystems; protected targets matched by real path; resolution revalidated at publication; atomic rename provides no compare-and-replace against external writers — that limit is stated, and stronger guarantees require host-coordinated publication. | P1.2.6 |
| D-48 | Production vs evaluation configuration (I-18) | **NEW** `Config.flags` holds production-optional features only; counterfactual research arms (reserve off, test-integrity off, delta-only, clamped routing, …) live in `EvalArms` of the `eval` module; production `AttemptConfig` validation rejects any combination that disables a mandatory control (reserve, test-integrity guard, Δ+absolute, floors, lifecycle controls); the evaluator may run such arms but marks them ineligible for promotion; freeze/version rules apply to both. | P0.1.3, P1.9.4, P6.1.2 |
| D-49 | Redaction and coverage (I-10) | **NEW** every `Observation` carries a redaction mask (source line ↔ rendered line mapping); redacted lines are excluded from anchored-edit coverage and from seed/recall-derived coverage; raw source version identity stays separate from the rendered observation; protected preimages never fill the gap. | P1.5.3, P1.6.3, P1.10.3 |
| D-50 | Report provenance and test identity (I-13) | **NEW** runner reports bound to `action_id`, runner/definition version, selector, completion status and captured-artifact provenance; fresh per-invocation report destinations (a build-cache path is allowed only with recorded provenance); structured evidence parsed before prompt shaping; test identity = (check, project/module, file/suite, name, parameterization) with multiplicity; missing/ambiguous evidence ⇒ `inconclusive`, never green. | P1.6.6, P1.7.5 |
| D-51 | Provider invocation identity and cancellation (I-15) | **NEW** caller-assigned `InvocationId` registered before dispatch; `ProviderAdapter.start(request, id): Invocation` with `await()`, `cancel()` and terminal observation; states requested → cancel-requested → provider-acknowledged → terminal-reconciled; late native output and usage archived exactly once and never executed; conservative unknown-usage hold until reconciled; an operation-correlation field, not a fifth identity. | P0.3.2, P0.3.4, P0.3.5 |
| D-52 | Contract slice content (I-11) | **NEW** the mandatory `[K]` slice and every review packet carry the complete applicable acceptance definitions (run command/selector, check/review text, origin, obligation version, requirement links) and, for weakened checks, the original obligation beside the diff; ids-only rendering is allowed only in the ≤ 150-token anchor digest; assessment acceptance stays with the verifier/user. Spec refinement of [§6.1](docs/context/compiler.md#sec-6-1) (§3.1). | P1.1.4, P2.3.1, P4.4.3 |
| D-53 | Snapshot byte fidelity (I-12) | **NEW** the snapshot authority is a raw-byte/type/mode manifest plus exact recovery blobs; Git trees/refs merely index them; blobs are written with `git hash-object --no-filters -w` (or restored from recovery blobs) so no attribute filter or line-ending conversion runs; the temporary index is initialized deliberately from the manifest; every materialized candidate is verified against its manifest before checks; the user's staged index is untouched; ignored/untracked inputs are accounted for explicitly; unsupported forms (submodules, sparse checkout) are rejected by name. | P1.2.3, P1.2.4, P1.7.5 |
| D-54 | Out-of-order graph component and frontier budget | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** owner-authorized P2.1.1 before the normal P0/P1 queue (§1.2), with dependencies and gates retained. `readyFrontier(contract, availableCells)` takes remaining cell slots after reservations; it is candidate selection, never token/money/lease admission. Depth is the longest prerequisite path. Cycles produce explicit SCC conflicts; joint increments may resolve requirement cycles. Requirement dependency producers must be reachable through increment prerequisites. Verified execution history stays regression-only; current ledger support requires work/attempt/contract identity, increment definition, originating cell and exact candidate stamp. P2.2 owns durable storage and admission; P3.1.2 owns broader reuse proofs. | P2.1.1, P2.2.2, P2.2.4 |
| D-55 | Out-of-order context selection kernel | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** P2.3.4 owns narrow immutable selection projections, using only P0.2.2 Tokens. Mandatory and already-present S/R roots require full dependency closure. Reject reachable cycles/missing dependencies for a root; mandatory faults prevent Fit. Choose feasible bundles by §6.1 priority, then sum of newly covered integer utility points / marginal K tokens (exact cross-products), then root ID; positive-gain zero-cost bundles rank first, zero-gain optional bundles are omitted. Recompute every round. Floor decimal alpha × profile tokens; arbitrary-precision sums retain overflow-sized capacity deficits. S/R unit costs are included in their fixed segment charges and validated against them. Unknown effective history prevents Fit. Policy `context-cover-v1`, assumptions, complexity and oracle are recorded in the journal. This is planning only; full P2.3.1/2/3 boundaries remain unchanged. | P2.3.4, P2.3.1–P2.3.3 |
| D-56 | Out-of-order calibration aggregation | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** P2.6.5 owns pure statistics over trusted supplied Increment/Sizing projections. Unit = repository/work/attempt/increment; identical deliveries deduplicate, conflicting checkpoints fail explicitly (including changed harness/policy metadata). Group by repository, harness version and sizing-policy version; subsystem is a caller-supplied stable label, null is an explicit unknown group. Completed and Failed terminal rows are eligible; Cancelled and Unfinished are censored with separate counts. expectedFiles=0 contributes to rates/medians but not ratios. Inclusive contiguous bands covering 0..Int.MAX_VALUE are caller-supplied and retained with aggregation-policy version, not new Config defaults. Ratios use DECIMAL128/HALF_EVEN, mean of individual ratios; median adds central Int values as Long. Strict warning compares 2×overruns > eligible count. No decay/window, empirical correction or routing change. Collection, DB, events and gated ≤150-token block remain P2.1.4/P2.6.4. | P2.6.5, P2.6.4 |

| D-57 | Out-of-order scorecard and paired inference | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** P6.1.4 owns immutable offline evaluation projections. Fixed trial-weighted estimand, repository clusters, complete declared pairs and config/matched-input digests; exact decimal weights, complex mass >= 0.5. Deterministic bounded-cluster Hoeffding lower bounds with family alpha split across two endpoints and all frozen candidates; no bootstrap or normality assumption. Conservative small-sample handling, strict -0.02 margin, separate unknown billing/floors/ceilings/integrity and synthetic-only results. Actual policy parameters and proof/simulation plan are in the [journal](audit/OUT-OF-ORDER-P6.1.4.md). This kernel never adopts a candidate; P6.1.1/2/3 and P7 retain their gates and responsibilities. | P6.1.4, P6.1.3 |

| D-58 | Out-of-order write-scope language algebra | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** P5.1.5 analyzes open write scopes, justified by existing create/rename-to-missing paths. Exact PathPattern semantics, Unicode scalar alphabet and explicit canonical lexical path language; one destination namespace. Tagged epsilon NFA, lazy subset/canonical-path product and iterative BFS provide shortest witnesses or a complete emptiness proof; limits and unsupported surrogate input give Unknown. Immutable scopes, deterministic alphabet tie order, existing-matcher witness validation. This neither authorizes physical writes nor enables S3; parents retain integration and gates. [Model and tests](audit/OUT-OF-ORDER-P5.1.5.md). | P5.1.5, P5.1.1, P5.1.4 |

| D-59 | Out-of-order constrained workload partition | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** P6.1.5 owns immutable metadata-only task/repetition/policy projections. Task-level exact decimal weight; explicit repository/family/extra must-links; half-open time windows; hard mass quotas and weighted absolute-deviation objective. DSU plus bounded iterative exact search; sound suffix bounds; immutable fingerprints; unknown metadata, invalid assignment, exhausted limits and proof of infeasibility stay distinct. [Frozen model/oracle](audit/OUT-OF-ORDER-P6.1.5.md). P6.1.2 retains contamination/holdout/manifest integration and FX-47. | P6.1.5, P6.1.2 |

| D-60 | Out-of-order exact DAG sequencing oracle | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** P4.5.5 consumes immutable graph and frozen context/cost projections. Only Pending selected increments, explicit completed external prerequisites, full mandatory precedence, pairwise/Markov-only costs. Exact decimal initial + switch + fixed charges, subset-DP and witness; deterministic mask/ID ties; bounded dense table/transitions with explicit ResourceLimit. [Model/oracle](audit/OUT-OF-ORDER-P4.5.5.md). No runtime activation or provider-savings claim. | P4.5.5, P4.5.3 |

| D-61 | Out-of-order finite attempt-policy costs | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** P4.5.4 owns immutable finite-state policies; one step per substantive attempt, explicit conditional/terminal/exhaustion charges. Exact decimal probability mass, rolling finite-horizon recurrence, unknown propagation, separate supplied eligibility/floors/conservative affordability before cost comparison. [Model and oracle](audit/OUT-OF-ORDER-P4.5.4.md). No learning, demotion or dispatch authority. | P4.5.4, P4.5.1, P4.5.2 |

| D-62 | Out-of-order exact trace analytics | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** P1.11.3 owns immutable trace snapshots, one WorkId/currency, dedup by payload; cost ancestry distinct from explicit exclusive execution/wait DAG. BigInteger nanoseconds, endpoint sweep, bottom-up costs and iterative longest path. Unknown money/clocks/open intervals/causal completeness preserved. [Model/oracles](audit/OUT-OF-ORDER-P1.11.3.md). No runtime accounting/emission/export completion. | P1.11.3, P1.11.1, P1.11.2 |

| D-63 | Out-of-order snapshot impact analysis | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** P3.2.7 owns qualified immutable graph/diff/check/anchor projections; reverse BFS permits cycles, check closures intersect blast, contract anchors intersect edits. Added+deleted line counts, duplicate dedup/overlap refusal, explicit unknown fan-in and broad package/workspace verification requirements. [Frozen model/oracle](audit/OUT-OF-ORDER-P3.2.7.md). Runtime producers/consumers and FX-37/54 remain with parents. | P3.2.7, P3.2.2, P3.2.5 |
| D-64 | Campaign disposition of a partial cell where the shape has no continuation cell | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P1.9.1). §3.7 continues a `partial` increment in a new cell (P2); S0 in P1 has none, so `Disposition.Continue` carries the honest fallback outcome: a cell turn/token/reserve stop ⇒ `budget_exhausted` (§5.9 "a budget stop is partial, never verified"); a pressure or stalled-completion stop ⇒ `failed` with the unavailable mechanism named (rebuild P2.5, gap-directed recovery P4.6), since neither is a budget. A refused `done` continues (fallback `failed`); a recovery-directed refusal stops `failed` until the ladder exists. Revisit when P2.2.2 adds continuation cells and P4.6 recovery. | P1.9.1, P1.9.3, P2.2.2 |
| D-65 | S0 eligibility without a pre-scan; which unknown outcomes block a relaunch | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P1.9.2). `ShapeSelector` (P1) selects S0 for one requirement, no `review:` item, no expected resume, one package (distinct `run:` cwd), no known contract touch or hard reversibility; an unassessed risk or file count (pre-scan P3.2.6) does not disqualify S0 but is reported as a limitation — never read as low risk (D-16) — and every mandatory control stays on. Anything else is `blocked_external("shape S1+ unavailable: …")`. `G_single(C)` that does not validate (no `run:` acceptance) opens no campaign: `waiting_for_input`. Open-time reconciliation marks every uncommitted intent `unknown_outcome`; the runner refuses to relaunch the same argv+cwd of an unknown intent unless both are `replaySafe` (R-class with argv-predictable effects, whose workspace effects the open-time tree reconciliation already covers, §13.1 "classified safe operations only"). Lifting a block is P2.2.4 resume reconciliation or authority. | P1.9.2, P2.2.1, P2.2.4 |
| D-66 | S0 controller outcomes where §3.7 names a P2 mechanism | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P1.9.3). `Controller.runS0`: `NEEDS_RESCOPING_OR_LARGER_PROFILE` ⇒ `blocked_external` (the host must split the request or grant a larger profile; `increment_split` is P2); `NEEDS_MORE_EVIDENCE` ⇒ `waiting_for_input`; an empty frontier with unverified requirements ⇒ `blocked_external` (never `completed`); `finish` re-certifies every `run:` item by a current receipt at the final stamp — a gap ⇒ `failed` naming it. The full suite (`CHK-full`, campaign-end trigger) is not run by the S0 finish: its receipt and `not_verified` belong to the finish receipt (P1.9.5), and pre-existing reds to the baseline ledger (P1.7.5). Syntax checking in the controller-built cell uses `CliSyntax` without interpreters (`not_run`) until the host names them. | P1.9.3, P1.9.5, P2.3.1 |
| D-67 | Default campaign budget when the host passes no policy | **PROVISIONAL** (P1.9.6). The documents give no campaign token default; `Astrolabe.campaign(project, request, policy = null)` budgets one full window of the main profile per allowed cell (`contextLimitTokens × Defaults.campaignCells`). A host that needs a tighter or money budget passes `CampaignPolicy`. Revisit with routing/economics (P4) and measured cost per accepted task. | P1.9.6, P1.11.2 |
| D-68 | Plan proposal intake: origin of approved acceptance; where proposals live | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P2.1.2). A plan-proposed item keeps `model(strengthens R)` whether frozen (autonomous: `Contracts.strengthen`, same version) or approved (interactive: one amendment per item through `Authority.resolve`, version bump, `obligationVersion` = the new version); the requirement's own `acceptance` list is not edited, so a requirement counts as judged when it has an item of its own or a `model(strengthens R)` item. Proposals are rows of `packets` (kind `plan`); the graph is admitted only after `validate` against the resolved contract, and a refused precheck changes nothing. Safe default: reversible (origin/packet kind are data). | P2.1.2, P2.1.5, P2.2.2 |
| D-69 | Weakening classification of a model amendment | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P2.1.5). `task.propose(amendment)` records `Contracts.propose(..., weakening = true)` whatever the model says: additions need no amendment (`strengthen`, plan acceptance proposals), so a model amendment may narrow, and the autonomous policy never auto-accepts it; the authority decides. Safe default; a host that wants finer classification needs a harness classifier (P3.4). | P2.1.5, P3.4.1 |
| D-70 | S1 loop choices where §3.7 leaves room | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P2.2.2). A new contract is stored with the shape its first selection gives (S1 unmasks `task.propose`); the plan cell runs under a `plan` pseudo-increment outside the graph and ledger; every `partial` continues the same increment from its carry-forward, bounded by `maxCells` (default 12, plan cell excluded) ⇒ `budget_exhausted`; a verified increment whose evidence went stale is re-accepted from receipts current at the stamp (regression obligation, never re-executed), else the campaign stops honestly; `NEEDS_RESCOPING` stops `blocked_external` naming the split the plan role should make. Reversible when P3/P4 add split replanning and the regression runner. | P2.2.2, P2.2.6, P3.1 |
| D-71 | Manifest `boundaryReason` for S1 cells and campaign-end regression re-runs | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P2.7.2). An implementing cell's manifest names why its context was built from the increment's previous cell: none or completed ⇒ `done`, partial or blocked ⇒ `partial`, failed/cancelled/still running ⇒ `resume`; the plan cell's manifest has none (`replan` waits for D-70 routing). §4.1 regression obligations are discharged by the harness: when the frontier is empty with stale verified increments, their `run:` acceptances are re-run once on the controller's authority (atlas rows as the unknown-closure inputs, as in a cell) and re-accepted from the new receipts; the increments are never re-executed. |
| D-72 | Counts of a passed end-of-turn checker receipt | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P3.1.4). A type/lint checker pass has no test count, but a passed receipt needs parsed counts (D-50): `CheckerResult.counts` = `Counts(errors = n)` when diagnostics were parsed, `Counts(discovered = files given to the checker)` for a pass (green only on exit 0, zero parsed errors and the tool's success signature; silent success only for eslint/tsc/go vet), else `null`. | P3.1.4 |
| D-73 | Isolated candidates for `slow\|expensive` checks | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P3.1.5). `Scheduler(candidates = layout.candidates)` exports the stamped tree (tracked ∪ untracked, raw bytes, declared scratch excluded) by copy under the mutation lock, verifies every copy, runs the check there without the lock, rescans content + metadata afterwards and deletes the copy; a failed export falls back to the exclusive protocol. The controller enables it only with `Flags.s3Writers`: with no concurrent writer, exclusive runs are equally eligible (D-45) and an export omits ignored dependency directories (`node_modules`, `.venv`, `target`) a real suite may need. ShadowRef materialization is not used, so extra snapshots never enter the drift ledger (FX-23). | P3.1.5, P5.1 |
| D-74 | "Justification on repeat" for crossing the increment write scope | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P3.4.1). Per cell (one `Edit` per cell): the first edit batch that touches paths inside the contract but outside the increment warns; every later batch that crosses it is refused (`scope`, nothing written) unless its `why` names each such path (full path or file name); `task.propose(increment_split)` is the other route, and a pending proposal grants nothing. | P3.4.1 |
| D-75 | Explicit refactor-mode flag | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P3.5.1). No new `Contract` field: a `Constraint` with id `refactor_mode` or text `refactor_mode[: true\|on]` activates refactor mode; `refactor_mode: false\|off` silences the labelled keyword heuristic (refactor/extract/rename/migrate API over requirement texts, the requests only while no requirement exists). | P3.5.1 |
| D-76 | Behaviour snapshot storage and scope | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P3.5.1). Captured once per attempt before the first cell in refactor mode: baseline receipts at `s0` over the affected suites (`CHK-full`, else unit/acceptance checks with a command) and characterization outputs by path convention (`golden*/`, `*.golden`, `__snapshots__/`, `fixtures/api/`; caps 200 files / 1 MiB) published as `OUTPUT` blobs; recorded as a `packets` row of kind `behaviour-snapshot` plus a Boundary journal line. | P3.5.1, P3.5.2 |
| D-77 | What `red_ok_until: increment_end` suspends | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P3.5.1). Only the register validator's "red not recorded" rule on `[>]` moves; inline syntax, the checker, the P3.1.4 step-boundary layer run and the repeated-failure gate keep running, and the exit gate still refuses a red completion. | P3.5.1 |
| D-78 | Non-approving campaign-review verdicts | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P3.5.2). `Reject` ⇒ campaign `failed`; `Revise`/`InsufficientEvidence`/`Escalate` ⇒ `blocked_external` (resumable, re-asked on reopen); no reviewer (`null`) ⇒ `blocked_external`, never skipped. | P3.5.2 |
| D-79 | New tests in the equivalence report | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P3.5.2). Tests present at `s_n` but not at `s0` do not break equivalence; they are listed as `newBehaviour` in the report and the review rubric (a new requirement is the reviewer's call), not a gate. | P3.5.2 |
| D-80 | CON-reference signal for interface-changing increments | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P3.5.2). Signal = `contract.contractsTouched` non-empty or the refactor checklist's `interfacesToChange` (not none/n/a); reference = a new `con` candidate or a `con_refs` id; with CON anchors in the KB the missing/unknown reference is a hard plan gap, otherwise a journaled planning gap. | P3.5.2 |
| D-81 | Reusing a signed campaign verdict | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P3.5.2). The campaign gate reuses an approving verdict obtained through `verify(review)` when stamp and contract revision are unchanged, refreshing the equivalence report; anything else asks again. | P3.5.2 |
| D-82 | Suites without test identities | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P3.5.2). A suite log that yields no identities (e.g. `pytest -q` dots) is `comparable = false` ⇒ not equivalent; counts never suffice, so a refactor repo needs `-rA` or JUnit XML for equivalence evidence. | P3.5.2 |
| D-83 | Precompile fingerprint breadth | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P3.7.1). Beyond the §6.6 list the fingerprint also digests the `[R]` prime, rules, calibration, estimator id/version, output limit and pinned messages (stricter, never looser); skills are an empty list until a skills producer exists (P4.4); increment identity = id + digest of title, requirements, accept, write scope, dependencies, expected files, produces. | P3.7.1 |
| D-84 | Precompile trigger | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P3.7.1). At the completion proposal, before verify-on-stop, when every due acceptance check is `slow\|expensive` (any fast/inline one due ⇒ skipped, journaled); a later proposal of the same cell supersedes the pending compile; the pre-built increment is `RequirementGraph.peekNext` (a prediction; a different frontier is caught by the fingerprint). | P3.7.1 |
| D-85 | Precompile telemetry | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P3.7.1). `PrecompileMetrics` records one sample per S1 boundary with the flag on (hit/miss/none, latency from cell close to the next `[K]`, nearest-rank p50/p95); hit/miss also journaled as Boundary lines; no new `AgentEvent`. | P3.7.1 |
| D-86 | Plan cell and precompile | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P3.7.1). Only implementing cells of the S1 loop trigger a precompile; the plan cell never does. | P3.7.1 |
| D-87 | Package identity and manifest edges | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P3.2.1). A file's package is the nearest ancestor holding a build manifest (`pyproject.toml`, `setup.py`, `package.json`, `build.gradle(.kts)`, `settings.gradle(.kts)`, `pom.xml`); id = that directory's workspace-relative path, the root package = the root directory name (`.` if unavailable or colliding), no manifest ⇒ unknown (workspace scope). Every file imports its package's manifests; Gradle `project(":x")`/`include` and Maven `<module>`/sibling `<dependency>` join manifests, so a build-script edit blasts through its module. | P3.2.1, P3.2.5 |
| D-88 | Import graph tier | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P3.2.1). Regex extraction ⇒ `ImpactGraph.tier = Lexical`, so the kernel never claims a complete blast and P3.2.5 always widens to package suites until tree-sitter (P5.4). `ImpactGraph.complete`/per-file `isComplete` is the extraction verdict: dynamic imports, reflection, generated markers and in-repo imports that fail to resolve ⇒ false with a description; clearly external imports (no in-repo root/namespace/relative specifier) stay complete. | P3.2.1, P3.2.3 |
| D-89 | Contract anchors before the KB | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P3.2.2). `ImpactAssembly` takes CON/ADR anchors as a supplied `ContractAnchors` list; `null` (nothing supplied) ⇒ `contractsComplete = false`, an empty list is an attested empty inventory. P4.1 loads them from the KB. | P3.2.2, P4.1 |
| D-90 | Hunk fan-in and interface change | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P3.2.2). The enclosing symbol is the innermost outline span containing the hunk's first new line; its fan-in is the `SymbolIndex.refs` count (tier/complete from the index, truncated ⇒ incomplete); a hunk outside every span uses the file's importer count, never complete. `interfaceChange` is a caller flag until the P3.2.4 outline diff produces it. | P3.2.2, P3.2.4 |
| D-91 | `blastChecks` projection | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P3.2.2). At tier 0 the kernel retains every check in `affectedTests` (D-63); `ImpactProjection.blastChecks` additionally reports the checks whose test files, naming targets or closure intersect the kernel's blast (the §7.4 union without the widening) for `look(impact)` and the nudge. Selection (P3.2.5) uses `affectedTests`, never `blastChecks`. | P3.2.2, P3.2.3 |
| D-92 | Impact nudge inputs and resolution | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P3.2.4). A changed definition = the tier-0 outline header (declaration lines up to the one opening the body, ≤8, whitespace collapsed) or its `exported` flag differs across the turn's edit batch (first preimage per path vs current bytes), or the definition is removed; kinds `signature`/`visibility`/`export`/`removed`; bodies never count. Fan-in = tier-0 lexical refs outside the edited file (also the no-index literal fallback). Resolution: an executed (`ok`) `look(refs)` naming the symbol (`name`, `Owner.name`, `path::name`) after the change, or a plan step added/rewritten naming it (rescoping). The exit gate binds only changed *public* (`exported` before or after) definitions; the controller's `Verifier.accept` does not re-check them (the cell gate refuses first). A reverted change stays pending until resolved. | P3.2.4 |
| D-93 | Blast check derivation and widening | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P3.2.5). `CHK-tests-blast` is derived from `CHK-full`'s command (none ⇒ `not_tested: blast radius: no test command declared by the repository`) and replaced (`Checks.replace`, last result kept) whenever `Verify` selects it: at a `[>]` move / integration layer and on `verify(tests(selection=blast))`, over the cell's touched paths. Exact selection (test files as arguments, `Closure.Known(tests ∪ blast)`, line `tests(blast N)` with N = files under test) only for a complete blast and a path-taking runner (`pytest`, `jest`, `vitest`, `mocha`); otherwise the package suite of the blast when it lies in one package with a directory (`cwd` = that directory, `Closure.Package`, `tests(package p)`), else the workspace suite (`Closure.Unknown`, `tests(workspace)`). Widening follows §7.3 (the blast's package), not the kernel's all-coverage scopes (D-63). A root-manifest package renders as `workspace`. | P3.2.5 |
| D-94 | Pre-scan candidates and fan-in | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P3.2.6). D-40 candidates: request tokens naming an atlas path (whole path or `/`-suffix, ≤20 matches); code-shaped identifiers only (backticked, called `f(`, snake_case, or an inner capital; ≥3 chars, never a plain word) resolved to their declaring paths via `SymbolIndex.def`; up to 3 hubs of the focus subsystem (top-level directories of those paths) ranked by importers in the import graph, manifests excluded. No candidate ⇒ `Prescan.UNKNOWN`. `filesEstimated` = the tier-0 blast (a lower bound), `crossPackage` only `true` (>1 known package) or unknown, `contractTouch` only `true` (KB `CON` anchors ∩ candidates) or unknown. `Prescan.fanIn` = the highest lexical refs count of a request identifier; with no declared risk, `fanIn ≥ largeMinFiles` raises risk to `medium`, never `high`, never lowers. Refresh after every cell return over inputs ∪ touched (journaled); a discovered contract touch stops S0/S1 `blocked_external` before commit. `contracts_touched` in the contract waits for KB loading (P4.1). | P3.2.6 |
| D-95 | Transform dispatch and receipt derivation | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P3.3.1). A transform is a batch of its own (refused `unsupported` with other ops). `script` runs as one shell line (as `run(cmd)`), `argv` as a direct launch; cwd = workspace root, env as `run`'s, deadline `TransformExecution.timeoutSeconds` (600 s). Authority is `run`'s: capability ceiling over the classified command, `Executors.require(executionMode)` (a confinement requirement ⇒ `unsupported`, D-41), a runner whose mode differs from the configured one ⇒ `unsupported`, a D-class classification ⇒ `unsupported` (D-class goes through `run(intent)`). The allowed inventory = tracked ∪ untracked workspace files matching `scope_glob` (`PathPattern`), scope-guarded before dispatch; `preconditions` are `path@version` pins (hex prefix) checked before dispatch; `inventory_ok` = changed files ⊆ declared inventory, `unspecified` when omitted. The diff = stamp diff ∪ in-scope files whose bytes moved; `match_count` = hunks of the harness's LCS line diff (binary or >4 000 lines ⇒ one hunk, listed under `limits`); representative sites = first hunk of the first, middle and last changed file; unusual = the three largest hunks whose (removed × added) shape is not the modal one. Per-file lines cap at 12 in the result; the full diff blob (`DIFF`) id is in `artifactRefs`. Non-zero exit, deadline or a lost observation reject the transform as a count miss does. | P3.3.1 |
| D-96 | Transform rejection without an isolated candidate | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P3.3.2). Transforms run in the cell's workspace; isolated candidates exist only behind `Flags.s3Writers` (D-73) and the edit tool never sees one, so the "discard the isolated candidate" branch is unreachable and the guarded inverse always runs (the branch returns with the S3 writer path). Effect derivation: `unknown_outcome` only when the script's observation was lost or cancelled; `partial` when any changed path is not restored — out-of-scope paths have no preimage and are listed as `outside scope_glob, no preimage recorded`, diverged in-scope paths as refused (FX-05); `restored` otherwise. Out-of-scope moves are announced to the registry from the stamp's before-member (else the recorded version) so stale reads drop. The rejected result's status wire is `rejected`, not `partial`: the receipt's per-file lines are the truth. | P3.3.2 |
| D-97 | Transforms in every shape | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P3.3.1). §3.5's table lists the tools as active in every shape and never withholds transforms; the S0 and S2 exclusions in `Roles.shapeMask`/`ToolOps.implementingS0` were P1 placeholders and are removed. Role masks and the capability ceiling still bound `edit.transform`; a cell without a `TransformExecution` answers `unsupported`. | P3.3.1 |
| D-98 | `touched-by-transform (NOT SEEN)` as a Workset entry | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P3.3.1). An accepted transform registers each changed, still-existing file as an `EntrySource.Transform` entry at its new version spanning every line with every line hidden (coverage ∅, D-49 style), `resultId = null` (a later stale drop says "read again"), 0 tokens; the Workset line renders them as `touched-by-transform (NOT SEEN): …` (a count in the short form), never as KNOWN, and the cell's displayed coverage ignores them. A rejected transform registers nothing. | P3.3.1 |
| D-99 | Stage C campaign fixtures: shadow ref in the cell's `edit`, declared acceptance closures, invariants asserted directly | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P3.8.2). (a) The controller now passes the campaign's `ShadowRef` to the cell's `Edit`, so `revert:turn:N` names the shadow snapshots the turn checkpoint records (before, every campaign `revert:turn:N` was `unsupported`). (b) `Checks.seed` still seeds every `CHK-accept-*` with `Closure.Unknown`; the migration fixture declares each run's closure through `Checks.replace` after open. Deriving it from `Acceptance.Run.scope` or the atlas is deferred to the owner of closure refinement (P3.1.1 follow-up / P4). (c) No invariant metric API exists; `StageCCampaignTest.invariantViolations` asserts the Stage C invariants on the records (no requirement lost or unverified, every verified increment certified by a current green receipt at the final stamp, no result served `current` off its own stamp without a reuse proof whose closure still matches the tree). A telemetry counter is deferred to P6. | P3.8.2 |
| D-100 | FX-36 reading and the lint's deterministic forms | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P4.1.1). FX-36 = a PIT from one failed use (one evidence ref) is kept as the scoped, conditional candidate it was proposed as: never rewritten, never a ban; its `global` or unconditional generalization fails the *not a one-off generalization* rule; the policy admits it only at `confidence ≤ 0.6`. Lint forms: dedupe = Jaccard ≥ 0.8 over summary words of one kind; evidence present = non-empty `basis.evidence_refs` resolving through `KbResolver`; anchors resolve in the tree; scope bounded = §4.5 vocabulary (`NoteScope`); contradiction = an admitted same-kind summary equal up to a flipped negation, or a second `CON` on an anchor `path#symbol` an admitted `CON` governs without `supersedes`; secrets = a `Redaction` hit (refused, never redacted in place). A lint failure rejects (a revision) in both modes. Interactive admission puts each lint-passing candidate to `Authority.resolve` as an `AmendmentProposal` whose id is the queue entry (no contract change, `weakening=false`); `Accepted` ⇒ `admitted_by`/`signed_by` = `byAuthority`, `Pending` ⇒ still queued. | P4.1.1 |
| D-101 | Admission batches without a new table | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P4.1.1). A batch is the set of `note_queue` entries carrying its id (no `note_batches` table, no schema bump); `rollback(batch)` returns each admitted note to `candidate` (a revision) and each entry to `queued` with `rolledBackFrom`, then regenerates the index — the tree is never touched. | P4.1.1 |
| D-102 | Prune, promote and health defaults | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P4.1.2). Decay = confidence × ½ per prune pass (unset counts 0.5) for LES/PIT/NEG/BMAP/SKILL injected ≥ 5 times and never cited; deprecated below 0.25. Promotion threshold = 3 citations; the note is superseded by a same-kind pointer note (`<id>-pointer`) and the task is a returned `Promotion` record, never committed or filed. Health: `harmfulInjections` is `null` until an outcome judge exists (P4.4); `repeatedMistakes` = admitted PITs cited from ≥ 2 cells; `indexFresh` = `kb/index` equals the current render. | P4.1.2 |
| D-103 | D-37 feature definitions, CAL handling and the ablation arms | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P4.1.3). Features in [0, 1]: `match` = 1 when an anchor lies in the write scope or the scope kind matches it (subsystem/path glob/task/roles), 0.25 for `global`; `overlap` = fraction of `depends_on` names among the contracts in play (admitted CON ids); `freshness` = 1 at the current stamp, 0.5 validated elsewhere, 0.25 never; `use_value` = cited/injected (0.5 before any injection); `evidence_quality` = min(1, refs/2), 1 when signed; length = tokens/500; threshold 2.0. Eligibility before ranking also excludes notes whose `depends_on` names a non-admitted note (FX-35 flag) and advice already injected in the cell (digest of id+summary+body). The CAL prior is mandatory for roles viewing `CalibrationPrior` and never served as global advice. `Flags.kbInjection`: `Off` (default) keeps F23 (CON/ADR in scope) and drops ranked advice and focus notes; `Frozen` ranks `OpenedCampaign.frozenNotes`; `Live` ranks the store. `kb.propose` is unmasked in every shape (D-97 style); the harness assigns the id. Consequence now live: an S0 cell touching a CON-anchored path ends the campaign `blocked_external` through the P3.2.6 pre-scan refresh (an ADR in the main line / S2 is required). | P4.1.3 |
| D-104 | Labelled negatives and usage rows | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P4.1.3). `retrieval_miss` (a `kb.search` with no hits) and `Open (needs: …)` lines are journal `nudge` events prefixed `retrieval_miss:` / `open-needs:`; `note_usage` holds one row per `(note, cell, event)` with `used_at = <instant> <event>`; citations are note ids found in the register render among the notes shown to the cell. No new tables. | P4.1.3 |
| D-105 | `task.delegate` requests name contract ids; the runtime copies the excerpts | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P4.4.1). The model's `delegate` packet carries requirement and constraint **ids**, uncertainties, scopes and a budget; `TaskPackets.assemble` copies the exact text and authority ref from the committed contract at its current version and refuses unknown ids. The model never authors an excerpt, so a child can only see contract text and never the parent's transcript (D13 enforced structurally). The dispatch candidate is the dispatching cell's own `Identities.candidate`. Non-writer kinds refuse a `writeScope`. | P4.4.1 |
| D-106 | Review cells at writer depth; writers S3-only; S0/S1 dispatch nothing | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P4.4.1). §10.1 gives writers depth 1 and probes depth 2 and says nothing about reviews; a review is an independent judge of the main line's work, so it sits at depth 1 with the writers. `task.delegate(writer)` "only in S3" (§3.4) is a kind rule in the `Delegator`, not a mask rule: the implementing mask lists `task.delegate/collect`, the shape mask admits them at S2+, and the `Delegator` refuses writers below S3 and every kind below S2. | P4.4.1 |
| D-107 | An excerpt is at most 4 000 characters | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P4.4.1). `Excerpt.MAX_CHARS = 4_000`: a requirement or constraint sentence never approaches it, and a bound keeps a transcript from being smuggled into a packet as one long excerpt. Raise it in `Excerpt` if a host's contracts carry longer clauses. | P4.4.1 |
| D-108 | Router candidates and tiers in the controller | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P4.5.1). A profile listed at a higher model tier serves a lower one (a tier is a floor; the argmin decides among them). The controller's candidates are the attempt's configured profiles under `Config.tierTable` when it tiers only configured profiles; otherwise (`TierTable.UNTIERED`, every fake-profile campaign) the supplied `CellModel.profile` serves every model tier through `TierTable.single`, so existing campaigns route to the profile they were given. Declared `Risk.contractTouch` is the "declared high" of D-34. Review is two rows (`ReviewCritical`, `ReviewRoutine`); a continuation's "same as the failing cell" is `RoutingPacket.previousTier`, tracked per increment in the S1 loop. "Escalate when" is row text for P4.5.2. User pins restrict the eligible set and never lower a floor; a set quality floor fails an unmeasured profile. | P4.5.1 |
| D-109 | Conservative estimate, affordability scope and calibration log persistence | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P4.5.1). The conservative estimate is one full-context turn: `context + output headroom` tokens and, at the profile's dated price table, uncached input plus output (an unpriced profile is unaffordable under a monetary cap). In the controller only a monetary budget (`Budget.cost` less its reserve fractions) caps affordability — tokens are admitted by the cell's own reservations (D-06), so the FX-49/FX-25 budget-exhaustion behaviour is unchanged; token affordability is exercised by the router's own FX-32 test. A refusal stops the campaign `BudgetExhausted` with the row's options. The expected total cost is the supplied `AttemptPolicy`'s (P4.5.4) when known, else the conservative estimate; unknown costs rank last. `CalibrationLog` is in-memory per controller (verified outcomes: `Completed` → accepted, `Failed` → verified failure, else unverified); durable persistence and the shadow-evaluation demotion evidence belong to P4.5.3/P6. | P4.5.1 |
| D-110 | The CAL delta's admission and supersession | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P4.2.1). The extractor only enqueues (one writer: the curator publishes), so the `CAL-<repo>` note is a queued candidate whose `origin.extractor` is `harness:<algorithm version>`; the autonomous policy admits a `CAL` candidate with that provenance as `admitted_by: harness` (D-36) and keeps a model-written `CAL` waiting (D-42: `kb.propose` sets `extractor = kb.propose`). A delta of an admitted `CAL` gets the next `CAL-<repo>-r<n>` id and names the admitted one in `supersedes`; admitting any candidate that names an existing note supersedes it (never a rewrite in place). The delta is enqueued only when the rendered body differs from the admitted one, and its evidence refs are the `campaign:<work>/<attempt>` rows the statistics came from. The extractor's tier and effort come from the §11.1 curation row without a `Router.selectProfile` call (no context to size; a calibration-log quadruple for extraction waits for a provider-backed extractor, P7). | P4.2.1 |
| D-111 | Derived candidates: scopes, recurrence and where the review's open items land | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P4.2.2). A derived `PIT` is scoped `subsystem:<directory>` of its anchor (the fact's anchor, the finding's location, else the packet's first change) and `task:<increment>` when no path is known, so the lint's scope rule holds and the policy can auto-admit it; its conditions clause is the record's own (`while implementing <increment>`, `when reviewed at <location>`, `under <dead-end scope>`), never invented. An unevidenced `x` fact or dead end derives nothing (evidence is a lint requirement, not a lint exemption). A repair diagnosis "recurs" when a dead end's normalized text appears in the registers of two or more cells of the campaign (the repair-helper cell of P4.5.2 will add its `Diagnosis` packets as a second source). Review findings ≥ major are derived once, with the campaign's last packet, and become `Open` items of the carried register at the next `carryFrom` (numbered after its last, deduplicated by text, `trip` = the location's path, `needs` = the suggested fix) — the "next register" of §8.8 is the continuation's or the reopen's carry, since a declined review stops the campaign. | P4.2.2 |
| D-112 | Skill record details, triggers, overlap rule and storage | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P4.3.1). `Skill` adds `invariants` (the §12.2 "invariants survive every filter") and `authority` (contract/ADR ids it follows, used only to decide overlaps; it grants nothing). The procedure head (trigger, prerequisites, invariants, steps, artifacts, verification, failure exit, freshness) and every mandatory module form the view's core, which no filter drops — the role's `appliesTo` included (the MB §8.5 counterexample); `appliesTo` and module-id/token filters act on optional modules only, and the role view spends the rest of `tokenBudget` on them in declared order; omissions are named (`role`/`filter`/`budget`). A trigger is path globs over the changed paths or case-insensitive terms in the change text, evaluated only at a `StateChange` (`IncrementOpened`, `FocusChanged`, `CheckFailed`, `ContractAmended`, `Replanned`), never per turn. Two triggered skills overlap when they claim a common changed path: the one citing the task's authority applies; otherwise both stay active and the conflict line is rendered as unresolved (follow the contract, report as Open). The whole skill is one content-addressed blob (`BlobKind.MODULE`) linked as the note's `procedure` module (no schema bump); a version is immutable (a changed body under one version is refused by the view cache). The compiler takes resolved skills in `CompileInputs.skills` (core mandatory, optional modules at `Skills` priority); the controller does not yet evaluate triggers into it (**P4.4.6**). | P4.3.1 |
| D-113 | Behaviour maps: index generation, locator states, disclosure and caps | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P4.3.2). From the tier-0 index a map has one behaviour per non-test source file with exports under the subsystem root: its exported declarations are the entry points, the file the implementation, importers outside the root the callers, test files targeting it the tests; state read/written is `unknown` to an index (`null`, distinct from none). A locator is `path::symbol@hash8` (atlas `hash8` at mapping time) and is validated against the current atlas at `look` and compile time: file or symbol gone ⇒ `unresolved`, other hash with the symbol still declared ⇒ `changed since mapped`, else current. Disclosure: `look(bmap, "<subsystem>")` renders the subsystem level capped at 400 tokens, `"<subsystem>#<behaviour>"` one behaviour; the symbol level is the locator and the source level is `look(read, path::symbol)`; every render states that a map never replaces current source, and no map result carries versions or registers coverage. A worker-observed behaviour is folded into the next map version only when all its locators resolve. The map is the note's `procedure` blob (D-112), scoped `subsystem:<name>`; a new version is a new note superseding the old. The `[R]` excerpt (`BehaviourMaps.excerpt`, ≤ 300 tokens) exists but the controller's prime has no focus subsystem yet, and the extractor's `BMAP_DELTA` is still a plain note (**P4.4.6**). | P4.3.2 |
| D-135 | Which §13.2 classes retry or repair, and where the §13.1 fences live | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P4.6.1). Only `Transport / rate limit` is `transient` (bounded retry, ≤ 2, backoff `1 s · 2^n`, and only for a classified-safe operation whose state is known); only `Build / environment` is `repairable` (one scoped capsule repair); every other class returns to its handler with the row's response, the evidence refs and the "must not conceal" text in the message. An unknown state or the unknown-outcome class reconciles first, whatever the class. `Ladder.recover` is a pure decision: it neither dispatches nor reconciles. The §13.1 fences are pure checks (`Fence.dispatch/publish/grant`): generation, lease authority and an atomic reservation before dispatch, generation and authority before publication (a refused publication still persists its observation), and no new writer while a previous owner's unknown effects are unreconciled; the live checks stay in `DispatchAuthority`/`Leases`/`Delegator`, and calling `Fence.grant` from `Leases.acquire` plus routing live failures into the ladder are the owning cell/controller's (P4.5.2). | P4.6.1 |
| D-136 | Guard budgets and what counts as no progress | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P4.6.2). A no-progress event is a failure whose full `Fingerprint` (signature, attempted fix, relevant state, requirement) was already seen anywhere in the campaign; the global budget is 3 such events (the first repeat nudges, the third trips: record a dead end or ask). The repeated-signature gate counts distinct attempted fixes per `ErrorSignature` (`repairs = fixes − 1`) and nudges once per count from `Defaults.repeatedSignatureRepairs` (2). The doom loop is per cell: the same tool and arguments `Defaults.doomLoopSameCalls` (3) times in a row under an unchanged caller-supplied state key (tree stamp plus observation sequence) trips. Per-tool error budget 8 failed calls per `family.op` per campaign; request cap `turnsPerCell × campaignCells` (480) model requests per campaign. The `ErrorSignature` is the operation plus the first error line made workspace-relative and normalized with `PreexistingLedger.normalize` (D-19). `Guards` is one in-memory object per campaign; feeding it from the cell loop and persisting it across resume are P4.5.2's (escalation) and P4.6.4's. | P4.6.2 |
| D-137 | Capsule repair: mask, acceptance re-check and where the diagnosis lands | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P4.6.3). The helper's tools are every op of the failing call's family plus `look.*` and `run.*` (`Capsule.mask`; `Roles.repair` copied with that mask per capsule); attempts are `min(Defaults.repairCalls, the §11.1 row's maxAttempts, 2)`; the profile is `Router.selectProfile(RepairHelper)` (low tier), a refusal escalates. A `fixed` claim is accepted only when the caller-supplied `OriginalAcceptance` holds on the tree afterwards; a failed re-check or a corrected call outside the mask is recorded in the next attempt's `previous` and never reported as fixed; after the last attempt the outcome is `Escalated`. Below S2, or with no remaining budget, the helper is not called and the outcome is `Escalated`. Every outcome's line (`repair <op>: …`, whole words, ≤ 100 tokens by the cell's estimator) is posted to the owner's `Diagnoses` inbox (`CellContext.diagnoses`); the cell renders the latest two posted lines in every later `[A]` beside the fired trips (never reduced). Who builds the capsule from a live failure and calls the helper (the cell on a `BuildEnvironment` `Recovery.Repair`) and the real repair cell behind `RepairRunner` are P4.5.2's/P4.4.6's (role text and packet validator). | P4.6.3 |
| D-145 | Mount effect class: what "locally approved validated read-only" means | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P4.7.1). `Mount.effectClassOverride` (host configuration) wins; otherwise a tool is `R` only when it is in `Mount.localApproval` **and** the server's own hints do not claim effects (`readOnlyHint == false` or `destructiveHint == true` withholds R); everything else is `D`. Hints can only lower trust, never raise it (F11): `readOnlyHint = true` alone is still `D`. Approvals and overrides must name described tools (construction fails otherwise). | P4.7.1 |
| D-146 | Mount invocation form and argument validation | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P4.7.1). `run(["mcp:<server>/<tool>", "<json object>"])`: argv form only (a `cmd` line, `bg`, `cwd` or more than one argument element is denied); a missing element means `{}`. Arguments are validated against the frozen `inputSchema`: a JSON object, every `required` key present, no undeclared key when `additionalProperties` is `false`; property types are the server's to check. A rejected call dispatches nothing. | P4.7.1 |
| D-147 | Mount capabilities under the caller's ceiling | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P4.7.1). Each `Mount` declares the capabilities an invocation needs (default `run-local`, a stdio server; a remote server adds `network`); the classification carries them into the same `Ceiling.allows(Classification)` as commands, so a mount never widens its caller (FX-39). Execution-mode confinement (D-11) and the D-class intent + authority step apply unchanged; a non-`R` mount call is `effects_unknown` and not replay-safe. | P4.7.1 |
| D-148 | Mount results, transport and catalog freeze | **LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE** (P4.7.1). The reply (`McpReply(content, isError)`) is redacted into a `LOG` blob and shaped by the generic shaper as exit 0/1, so a successful call is `inconclusive`, never `passed` (a reply is data, not test evidence); a thrown transport error is `unknown_outcome` with the intent left open; no transport configured ⇒ `unavailable`, nothing dispatched. `Catalog` snapshots its mounts at construction and exposes a `digest`; a server that re-describes itself needs a new catalog (new session). `look(catalog)` appends `mounted mcp:<server>/<tool> [class] — <first description line, ≤100 chars>`. `McpClient` is the transport interface only (P7); its Java form is `JavaMcpClient` (`CompletableFuture`; cancelling the waiter cancels the future). Nothing constructs a `Catalog` from `Config` or the controller yet (host/P7 wiring). | P4.7.1 |

### 3.1 Specification refinements recorded by this plan
The plan follows the refined reading below; a later documentation-maintenance pass should apply them to the cited sections (they are not silent redesigns — each keeps the section's intent and closes an ambiguity found in review):
- [§8.4](docs/verification/scheduler.md#sec-8-4): `current = (stamp_after == stamp_now)` is necessary, not sufficient; add `tested_inputs`/`input_stability` (D-45, I-04).
- [§6.1](docs/context/compiler.md#sec-6-1) and [§5.1 `[K]`](docs/runtime/context-layout.md#sec-5-1): "acceptance ids and kinds" → complete acceptance definitions with origin and obligation version (D-52, I-11).
- [§5.4 look dedup](docs/runtime/tools.md#sec-5-4) and result ids: dedup needs semantic request equivalence + live coverage; `#n` aliases are campaign-global (D-46, I-06/I-07), consistent with [§6.2](docs/context/continuity.md#sec-6-2) cross-cell recall.
- [§5.1/§5.4 "masked, never removed"](docs/runtime/context-layout.md#sec-5-1) is governed by [§15.1](docs/platform/adapters.md#sec-15-1)'s adapter qualification (D-20, I-24).
- [§14.3](docs/platform/security.md#sec-14-3): "authorized, version-pinned rules snapshot" — pinning ≠ authorization; trust is bound by host policy (D-32, I-08).
- [§8.7](docs/verification/acceptance-review.md#sec-8-7) exit gate: "red" means a receipt whose outcome is `failed`. An inconclusive, timed-out or unavailable check is **missing evidence, not red** (L8): it refuses completion where the check is required, and needs no Open item where it is not. Conflating the two blocked every S0 completion, because a generic shaper returns `inconclusive` until P3.1.4 (P1.8.7, 2026-09-21).

## 4 Phases

Legend per phase header: **Fixtures** = FX/AX rows that must pass as harness tests before the phase closes · **Unsupported until later** = assumptions the phase does not validate.

---

## P0 Foundation — project, contracts, hooks, storage, OS
Goal: scaffolding that establishes real contracts and boundaries; nothing here talks to a model or a repo yet. Mirrors [§3.2 minimal forms](docs/architecture/components.md#sec-3-2) and the "resolve ownership and public record/tool contracts first" instruction of the map.
**Fixtures:** AX-01..AX-10 at contract level (fake adapter + `validate()`); their runtime consequences are re-verified in the phases the §5 AX table names · **Unsupported until later:** any provider parity; confined execution.

### P0.1 Build and conventions

#### P0.1.2 [V] CI matrix · DONE
- Build: workflow running `gradlew check` on Linux and Windows (D-12); caches Gradle; publishes test reports.
- Done: both jobs green on the skeleton.
- Log: 2026-09-20 — .github/workflows/ci.yml: ubuntu-latest + windows-latest matrix, Temurin 26, gradle/actions/setup-gradle, gradlew check, test reports uploaded. Not yet executed on a remote CI runner (no remote configured in this session); local Windows run green.
- Log: 2026-09-20 targeted audit — workflow implemented, acceptance not met: two failed remote runs; latest 35514932596 at 468f5b0 failed on both platforms. Linux never started tests (gradlew mode 100644, Permission denied, exit 126); Windows core: 758 tests, 3 failures, 130 skips. Reopened IN_PROGRESS; resolve with P0.6.1/P0.6.2/P0.6.4 and obtain green checks on both platforms. See §1.1.
- Log: 2026-09-21 — all four recorded failures diagnosed from run 35514932596's own logs and test-report artifact (not from reruns) and repaired; §1.1 carries the evidence. (1) Linux: `gradlew` is recorded `100755`; `SkeletonTest` asserts the recorded mode so a Windows-only checkout cannot regress it. (2) `GitTest` index refusal was a **product defect**: the D-53 guard compared path *spellings*, and the runner's short `TEMP` name differs from `rev-parse --absolute-git-dir`, so the user's index passed the guard; `Git.requireTemporaryIndex` now compares canonical paths and also protects the common dir's index (P0.6.2). (3) `FixtureReposTest` TS filename: Node 22's junit reporter emits no `file` attribute, so the D-27/D-50/IX-13 disambiguation the fixture asserts does not exist below Node 24; that assertion moved to its own test guarded by `Runners.nodeMajor()`, and CI pins Node 24 (P0.6.4). (4) `ProcOwnershipTest` grandchild: the fixed 10s execution deadline was shorter than the launcher's own start-up on the runner (~20s, read from the artifact's per-test times), so the tree died before the grandchild existed; the deadline is now sized from a measured `ChildCommands.launcherReady()` probe (P0.6.1). CI additionally installs ripgrep on both runners: 128 of the 130 Windows skips were `rg is not on PATH`, which left P0.6.3's both-backends criterion unexercised on CI. Local Windows/JDK 26 full build: core **878 tests, 0 failures/errors, 6 existing platform skips**, eval 30, provider-api 15. Acceptance still requires both remote jobs green.

- Log: 2026-09-21 (closed) — [CI run 35623690223](https://github.com/korvin2000/ASTROLABE/actions/runs/35623690223): both jobs green at `8052946`: Ubuntu in 2m02 with three platform skips (the Windows-quoting test and the two junction tests), Windows in 8m45 with two (the POSIX session test and the unreadable-file denial). ripgrep 14.1.0 / 15.2.0 and Node 24 on both, so the search parity suite and the TS junit fixture really ran — the 130 Windows skips of the reopening audit are gone. P0.1.2 is DONE; P0 reaches `FIXTURE_VALIDATED` (the phase's harness tests pass on Windows and Linux). Every live gate stays `UNMEASURED` (P7).
#### P0.1.3 [C] `Config` and `Defaults` · DONE
- Why: every number of [§17](docs/reference/defaults.md#sec-17) is configurable per task and none is hard-coded; harness changes take effect only at attempt boundaries (invariant 12).
- Pkg: root `io.astrolabe`
- Build: `Defaults` with one named field per §17 row (shape policy, `turnsPerCell = 40` with the 80 % nudge, `alpha = 0.65`, `k = 8`, `m = 6`, `rMaxTokens = 16_000`, `anchorMaxTokens = 2_500`, `immediateStubTokens = 800`, `lookBudget = 1_500`, `runBudget = 1_200`, `registerCap = 1_200`, `digestCap = 150`, `patchCap = 400`, fact/note/summary caps, seeds/injection/focus caps, `touchedInAnchor = 10`, `checkerTimeBoxSeconds = 20`, `theta = 40`, `fullSuiteCadence = 5`, reserves, guard counters, probe/review/repair/attempt/depth/parallel limits, `campaignCells = 12`, flaky policy, admission policy, profiles, mode/execution mode/`dClass`/ceiling, `runTimeoutSeconds = 120`); `Config(defaults, mode, executionMode, rulesFile (trust binding per D-32), stateRoot (D-44), flags: one switch per production-optional `[O]` mechanism only)`; sections owned by later tasks are added there, not here (roles + texts P1.8.1/D-38, redaction P1.10.3, tierTable P4.5.1, injectionWeights P4.1.3/D-37) — I-01; counterfactual research arms live in `EvalArms` of the `eval` module, never in `Config` (D-48); `AttemptConfig` (harness version, config snapshot, profiles, role-text versions) with a validator that rejects any combination disabling a mandatory control; frozen at campaign open (P1.9.4).
- Done: a test enumerates the §17 table against `Defaults` fields; every `[O]` flag defaults off; the validator rejects a reserve-off or test-integrity-off production config (IX-18).
- Log: 2026-09-20 — Defaults (one field per §17 row, 25 rows; DefaultsTest maps the table to the fields both ways so no unowned number exists), ShapePolicy (D-16 classes, s3Enabled=false, slackFactor 1.5), ProfileRoles, Config(defaults, profiles, profileRoles, mode, executionMode, dClass, ceiling, rulesFile: RulesBinding (D-32), stateRoot, flags: Flags = 13 [O] switches, all off by test), ConfigViolation; AttemptConfig(harnessVersion, config, roleTextVersions, controls: Controls, production) with a @Transient fingerprint over stable JSON; freeze() throws InvalidConfig on any production violation (reserve 0, checker time box 0, attempts 0, unknown profile…); researchArm() is the EvalArms entry (production=false, never promotion-eligible) — IX-18. Declared owning-package enums early: auth.ExecutionMode, auth.Stage (extended by P1.10.x), route.Tier (extended by P4.5.1), root Mode/DClassPolicy. Astrolabe.VERSION = 0.1.0.
